Washington Cannabis & Hemp — Audited Final Report

Last audited: 1 November 2026  |  Research cut-off: 1 November 2026  |  Parts 0–12, complete, with audit applied in place

Single self-contained HTML file. Open it offline. Part 1 below is the audit. Part 2 is the full rewritten report — every section and all detail from the original are retained; only what the audit changed has been changed. Where a body line in Part 2 still conflicts with an audit row in Part 1, the audit row prevails. No 3 November 2026 election result is in this file (cut-off 1 November 2026).

Audit method and limits — read first

Primary sources only were used to confirm or correct: the Revised Code of Washington and bill pages on leg.wa.gov, the Washington Administrative Code and Washington State Register, the Liquor and Cannabis Board (licensee lists, downloads, annual reports, enforcement/recall material), the Department of Health, WSDA, the Department of Revenue, the Governor's office, the text of tribal cannabis compacts, the Secretary of State for ballot measures and certified results, Washington courts, official tribal government sources, and official county and city sites and codes. News, law-firm blogs, trade press and AI summaries were not used as confirmation; where only a secondary source exists the verdict is Unverified. The federal baseline in the user's brief was used as given and was not re-audited; only the report's statements against it, and Washington's responses to it, were checked. No statement in the report was found to contradict the federal baseline, except that the report correctly flagged — and this audit retains as Verify — whether LCB medical-endorsement sales and tribal-compact medical activity count as 'state-licensed medical' under 91 FR 22714.

Limit: several direct page opens were rate-limited (429) during the audit and were not retried, and LCB download rows are not exposed in text fetch. Those items stay Verify in the table below and in the 'still unverified' list, with the exact live-browser or public-records route for each. LCB direct file URLs/filenames are therefore not guessed in this file. No personal phone number or email for an individual licence holder is reproduced anywhere in this file.

Verdict counts (audit rows sum exactly): 125 rows — Confirmed 58, Corrected 40, Removed 1, Unverified 26. 'Confirmed' includes rows confirmed with a source caveat or a date/wording precision correction where the substance stands; 'Corrected' means the report's figure, date, citation or attribution was wrong and a correct version is given; 'Removed' means wrong with no correct current version (the 46-reserve figure, and the $1,480/$1,200 research-fee figures as current figures, removed inside the Corrected research-fee row); 'Unverified' means no primary source was opened that settles it.

Federal baselineWhether LCB medical-endorsement sales count as 'state-licensed medical' under 91 FR 22714Unverifiedhttps://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=69.50.375Keep flagged Verify. The endorsement is an endorsement to a retail licence (RCW 69.50.375), not a separate medical licence; the federal rule text as given does not settle it.
Federal baselineWhether tribal-compact businesses count as 'state-licensed' under 91 FR 22714Unverifiedhttps://lcb.wa.gov/tribal/cannabis_compactsKeep flagged Verify. Compact activity is not under ch. 69.50 RCW. Do not assert tribal businesses are state-licensed.
Parts 0-137% excise on selling price of concentrates, useable cannabis and cannabis-infused products at retail, RCW 69.50.535; separate from and not in the sales-tax baseConfirmedhttps://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=69.50.535Keep. Sales tax is computed on the pre-excise price.
Parts 0-1Adult possession/transaction: 1 oz useable, 16 oz solid infused, 72 oz liquid infused, 200 mg low-dose liquid, 7 g concentrateConfirmedhttps://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=69.50.360 ; https://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=69.50.4013Replace 'lawful ceiling in practice' with the statutory rule: RCW 69.50.4013(3)(a) — possession within RCW 69.50.360(3) limits is not a violation; sale must be on retail premises.
Parts 0-1No adult home grow; unlicensed manufacture is a class C felonyConfirmedhttps://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=69.50.401 ; https://app.leg.wa.gov/rcw/default.aspx?cite=69.50.430Keep in substance. Add: RCW 69.50.430(1)-(2) additional fines $1,000 first felony conviction, $2,000 second/subsequent, not suspendable/deferrable unless indigent. Class-C maximum years/dollars (ch. 9A.20) remains Verify.
Parts 0-1Simple possession over limits described as a '>40 g felony'Correctedhttps://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=69.50.4013 ; https://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=69.50.4014Do not use '>40 g felony'. Knowing possession under §4013 is a gross misdemeanor (up to 180 days/$1,000; up to 364 days/$1,000 with 2+ priors after 1 July 2023); §4014 makes knowing possession of ≤40 g a misdemeanor.
Parts 0-1Gifting: half amounts, cited to RCW 69.50.357, flagged VerifyCorrectedhttps://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=69.50.4013Cite RCW 69.50.4013(4): in a single 24-hour period, noncommercial delivery 21+ to 21+, not for consideration, of 1/2 oz useable, 8 oz solid, 36 oz liquid (100 mg THC if ≤4 mg/unit), 3.5 g concentrate; out of public view in a nonpublic place, or in original retail packaging.
Parts 0-1Patient/co-op limits: card 3 oz / 48 oz / 216 oz / 21 g; grow 6 / 15 / 4 plants; possess 8 / 16 / 6 ozConfirmedhttps://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=69.51A.210Keep. Higher-need retail limits do NOT rise: §210(2) specifies grow/possession only; database-patient transaction limit stays 3 oz / 48 oz / 21 g / 216 oz.
Parts 0-1Cooperatives: max 4 members, 60 plants, 72 oz at locationConfirmedhttps://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=69.51A.250Keep. Statute controls over any DOH '8 oz' per-member shorthand. One co-op per member, one registered location, one per tax parcel, 60-day wait after a departure.
Parts 0-1Medical endorsement, Database, recognition card, $1 fee, validityConfirmedhttps://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=69.50.375 ; https://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=69.51A.230Keep, reworded: $1 statutory department fee per initial/renewal card, collected by the retailer; DOH notes stores may charge more — not a statutory floor. Cards valid 1 year (18+) / 6 months (under 18). Database information shall not be shared with the federal government unless the patient/provider is convicted in state court under ch. 69.51A/69.50.
Parts 0-1Qualifying conditions (a)-(f) plus '(g) approved, PTSD/TBI Verify'Correctedhttps://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=69.51A.010Add as statutory conditions: (g) posttraumatic stress disorder, (h) traumatic brain injury. Delete the Verify. Qualifying patient must be a WA resident at diagnosis.
Parts 0-1Out-of-state patients / no reciprocityUnverifiedhttps://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=69.51A.010Keep as secondary-supported, not primary-confirmed. WA residency at diagnosis is primary-confirmed, which entails no out-of-state medical qualification; visitors 21+ may still buy adult-use amounts.
Parts 0-1Medical excise exemption (until 30 June 2029, endorsed retailer, recognition card, DOH-compliant ch. 246-70 product tested to WAC 246-70-040) and sales-tax exemptionConfirmedhttps://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=69.50.535 ; https://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=82.08.9998Keep. DOR gloss that any product bought by a card holder is 'compliant' conflicts with §9998(1)(a) ch. 246-70 test — keep that gloss Unverified, statute controls.
Parts 0-1SB 5123 (2023) employment protections, effective 1 Jan 2024Confirmedhttps://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=49.44.240Keep, with limits: initial hiring only; exemptions include federal clearance, general-authority law enforcement, fire, first responders/911/EMS, corrections, airline/aerospace, and employer-pre-identified safety-sensitive (substantial risk of death) positions. Does not affect post-accident/suspicion testing or federal obligations.
Parts 0-1 / 7SB 5367 / E2SSB 5367 (ch. 365, Laws of 2023, eff. 23 July 2023): any product intended to be consumed/absorbed with any detectable THC is a cannabis product, LCB-channel only; synthetically derived/completely synthetic cannabinoids barred except FDA-approvedConfirmedhttps://lawfilesext.leg.wa.gov/biennium/2023-24/Htm/Bills/Session%20Laws/Senate/5367-S2.SL.htm ; https://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=69.50.3251Keep in substance. General-retail hemp delta-9 edibles/drinks, delta-8 and THCA smokable flower remain LCB-only.
Parts 0-1 / 7Hemp definition: 0.3% total THCCorrectedhttps://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=15.140.020Washington law is delta-9 only: RCW 15.140.020(7) — Cannabis sativa L. with delta-9 THC not more than 0.3% on a dry-weight basis. Total THC is a USDA testing concept, not the RCW 15.140 definition.

Parts 2280E and Washington ch. 82A: 'no state-level 280E relief'Correctedhttp://apps.leg.wa.gov/rcw/default.aspx?cite=82A.04.350&pdf=trueRCW 82A.04.350 (2026 c 238 s 313) confirmed: in computing WA taxable income, a taxpayer may deduct expenditures disallowed under §280E so long as they relate to commercial cannabis activities by a person licensed under RCW 69.50.325. Chapter 82A is an Income Tax chapter — this is NOT B&O relief and NOT general WA decoupling. Who owes ch. 82A tax (thresholds) and from what tax year remain Verify. 'No state-level 280E relief for B&O' remains correct for B&O only.

(11) allows only retailer transport to its own locations or return to a processor under WAC 314-55-085. No retail-to-consumer delivery licence or medical-delivery exception was located.
Parts 2 / 3 / 111,000-ft buffer reducible to 100 ft except schools/playgrounds — blanket statementCorrectedhttps://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=69.50.331Keep with two corrections: (8)(b) reduction proviso — the reduction must not negatively impact civil regulatory enforcement, criminal law enforcement interests, public safety, or public health. Research premises under RCW 69.50.372 MAY be reduced to 100 ft even from schools/playgrounds by ordinance, if they exceed producer/processor/retailer security standards, are inaccessible to the public with no part in public view, and bear no cannabis-research signage. Buffer measurement (WAC 314-55-050(2)) remains Verify.
Parts 2 / 4Annual licence fee $1,781 'from 1 July 2026 (EHB 2681)', was $1,381Correctedhttps://lawfilesext.leg.wa.gov/biennium/2025-26/Pdf/Bills/Session%20Laws/House/2681.SL.pdf?cite=2026%20c%20262%20s%201 ; https://lcb.wa.gov/sites/default/files/2026-07/EHB2681_CR103_Memo.pdfCorrect everywhere to: EHB 2681 (ch. 262, Laws of 2026), signed 1 Apr 2026, statute effective 11 June 2026; LCB WAC alignment (WAC 314-55-075/-077/-079) effective 1 July 2026. $1,381 → $1,781 producer/processor/retailer annual issuance/renewal; $250 application unchanged; CPI (Seattle CPI-U) adjustment from 1 July 2027. $1,381 is stale after 1 July 2026.
Parts 3 / 11Signage conflict: 4 signs (ESB 5206, WSR 26-12-082, eff. 4 July 2026) vs LCB FAQ 2 signs / 1,600 sq in — left as conflictCorrectedhttps://app.leg.wa.gov/wac/default.aspx?cite=314-55-155 ; https://lawfilesext.leg.wa.gov/biennium/2025-26/Htm/Bills/Session%20Laws/Senate/5206.SL.htmCONFLICT RESOLVED in favour of 4 signs. Current WAC 314-55-155(3)(a): a retailer may display four separate signs, not exceeding 1,600 sq in EACH, on the main-entrance side; (4) adds, in addition to the four, billboards and up to two trade-name signs under RCW 69.50.369. The FAQ '2 signs' figure is the pre-ESB 5206 rule / trade-name-sign count only — do not quote it as the total. ESB 5206 = ch. 378, Laws of 2025, statute eff. 1 Jan 2026; rule WSR 26-12-082 filed 3 June 2026, eff. 4 July 2026.
Parts 11Advertising bans, 1,000-ft ad ban, out-of-state targeting ban, coupon floorConfirmedhttps://app.leg.wa.gov/wac/default.aspx?cite=314-55-155Keep, with citation correction: (2)(a) bans false/misleading, overconsumption, alcohol/tobacco/motor-vehicle portrayals, LCB-mimicry, curative/therapeutic claims and youth-appealing material; (2)(b)(i) 1,000-ft ad ban (unless local requirement was reduced by ordinance); (2)(c) 21+ text except trade-name signs; (2)(d) out-of-state targeting ban; (5)(g) coupons may not discount below acquisition cost. The report's '(3)(c) publicly owned/operated property' citation is wrong in current text — (3)(c) lists arenas, stadiums, shopping malls, state-allocation fairs, farmers markets and video-game arcades.
Parts 11Giveaways / promotional items under older WAC 314-55-155(4) + Bulletin 21-01 item listCorrectedhttps://app.leg.wa.gov/wac/default.aspx?cite=314-55-155Current (5): giveaways/branded or unbranded merchandise banned except incidental items that qualify as paraphernalia under RCW 69.50.102, are advertised only by in-store merchandising, were bought at fair market value, bear only the retailer's branding, are worth < $1, and are not conditional on another purchase under WAC 314-55-017(2). 'Giveaway' excludes non-infused edible/topical samples offered on retail premises for sampling only. Do not carry old (4) numbering or the bulletin item list as current rule text without re-opening the bulletin. Medical-endorsement donation rule (6) Confirmed verbatim.
Parts 11Insurance $1,000,000 CGL, WAC 314-55-082 — flagged Verify (direct open rate-limited in Run 2)Confirmedhttps://app.leg.wa.gov/wac/default.aspx?cite=314-55-082REMOVE the Verify flag. 'The limits of liability insurance will not be less than $1,000,000' for CGL or commercial umbrella, at all times, proof on Board request; carrier authorised in WA, A- Class VII or better in Best's Reports, else ch. 48.15 RCW / 284-15 WAC; State + employees/agents/volunteers named additional insured, policies primary. Failure to maintain/prove may result in licence cancellation. WSR 22-21-058, filed 12 Oct 2022, eff. 12 Nov 2022. This is insurance, NOT a surety bond — do not present $1,000,000 as a bond.
Parts 11Waste 50% non-cannabis mix, WAC 314-55-097Confirmedhttps://app.leg.wa.gov/wac/default.aspx?cite=314-55-097Keep, date corrected: current history WSR 25-07-049, filed 12 Mar 2025, eff. 12 Apr 2025 (not 13 Apr 2025). (4)(a): grinding + mixing so mixture is at least 50% noncannabis waste by volume. Sellable waste (≤0.3% THC, not dangerous/hazardous, excluding hemp as defined in RCW 15.140.020): to a non-licensee — report to WSDA + notify LCB before sale, quantity/price/buyer name+contact, open and non-discriminatory sale, will not leave Washington; to another producer/processor — both notify LCB with quantity/price/intended use.
Parts 11Sampling table, WAC 314-55-101 — Verify, two competing tablesCorrectedhttps://app.leg.wa.gov/wac/default.aspx?cite=314-55-101Verify RESOLVED, older table REMOVED. Current (3): samples ≥1 g each, roughly equal weight; up to 10 lb = minimum 8 samples; 10 to <20 lb = 12; 20 to <30 lb = 15; 30 to <40 lb = 18; 40 to 50 lb = 19. The older '5 lb lot / 4 samples' presentation is not current rule text — delete it. Labs must reject/fail samples not collected as required, adulterated, contaminated or manipulated.

Parts 11Traceability: BioTrack → Leaf → CCRS as a seed-to-sale systemCorrectedhttps://leg.wa.gov/jlarc/reports/2025/CannabisMarket/pf_ii/default.htmlJLARC July 2025: BioTrackTHC 2013-2017; Leaf replaced BioTrack in 2017, never fully deployed, multiple bugs/errors; CCRS replaced Leaf in 2021, LCB-developed as a temporary reporting solution — 'It is not a traceability system' and its data were found incomplete/unreliable. Correct to: BioTrack (2013-2017) → Leaf (2017-2021) → CCRS (2021-present, a reporting system, not a full seed-to-sale traceability system). Month-level cutover dates (Dec 2021-Jan 2022, Jan 2023 manifest update) remain secondary-only.

Parts 12Driving: 5 ng per se, RCW 46.61.502; under-21, RCW 46.61.503; physical control, RCW 46.61.504; test evidence, RCW 46.61.506Confirmedhttps://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=46.61.502 ; https://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=46.61.503 ; https://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=46.61.504 ; https://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=46.61.506Keep, distinguished: 502(1)(b) THC 5.00+ within 2 hours by blood under 506 is a per se prong, distinct from (1)(c) under-influence/affected and (1)(d) combined influence; 502(6) enhancement is class B felony, 504(6) is class C. Under-21 503 is '>0.00 but <5.00', its own misdemeanor, not a stand-alone zero-tolerance 502 offence. 506 caution: the page opened is headed 'Effective June 30, 2027' (2026 c 108 §1 expires 30 June 2027) — at republication cite the in-force version.
Parts 12Vehicle possession/consumption, RCW 46.61.745 — 'Verify verbatim text'Confirmedhttps://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=46.61.745Verify RESOLVED. Traffic infraction to keep cannabis in a vehicle on a highway unless (A) in the trunk, (B) in another area not normally occupied / not directly accessible if no trunk, or (C) in an unopened package/container with seal unbroken and contents not partially removed; a glove compartment / utility compartment is deemed within the area occupied by driver/passengers. Consuming cannabis in any manner in a vehicle on a public highway is a traffic infraction.
Parts 12Public consumption, RCW 69.50.445Confirmedhttps://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=69.50.445Keep: opening/consuming cannabis / usable cannabis / infused products / concentrates in view of the general public or in a public place is unlawful; 'public place' per RCW 66.04.010, exclusions in 66.04.011 do not apply; class 3 civil infraction under ch. 7.80 RCW.
Parts 12Vacation, RCW 9.96.060(5) / SB 5605 (2019) — multiple-conviction scope Verify; limits 'single conviction, 1998-2012, state law only' attributed to SB 5605Correctedhttps://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=9.96.060Scope RESOLVED from current text: (5) 'Every person convicted of a misdemeanor cannabis offense, who was 21 years of age or older at the time of the offense, may apply to the sentencing court for a vacation' — including §69.50.4014 from 1 July 2004 onward and predecessors; 'If an applicant qualifies under this subsection, the court shall vacate.' (5) as written states no single-conviction limit, no 1998-2012 window, and no state-law-only limit. Those three limits belong to the Governor's pardon Initiative, NOT to SB 5605. SB 5605 = ch. 400, Laws of 2019, eff. 28 July 2019. Treatment-completion vacatur (6) Confirmed (court must vacate). Felony vacation under RCW 9.94A.640 remains Unverified; no statewide vacated-convictions total was located — keep Unavailable.

How to read · Reconciled figures — read first · Parts 0–1 · Parts 2–3 · Parts 4–5 · Part 6 · Parts 7–8 · Parts 9–10 · Parts 11–12 · Research notes (both runs)

How to read this file, and the federal baseline (given, not re-researched)

Federal baseline, used as fixed context: split scheduling — state-licensed MEDICAL marijuana and FDA-approved products moved to Schedule III effective 28 April 2026 (91 FR 22714); all adult-use marijuana remains Schedule I; the broader 2024 rescheduling proposal is not final. IRS 280E is generally lifted for Schedule III medical activity; adult-use remains fully subject to 280E. Hemp: PL 119-37 §781 takes effect 12 November 2026, but PL 119-103 §2019 limits it until 11 December 2026; after 11 December 2026 the full total-THC hemp definition and the 0.4 mg total THC per container cap apply. USDA enforcement of DEA-registered lab testing for hemp is delayed to 31 December 2026. The federal medical-marijuana appropriations rider runs through 11 December 2026. There is no federal cannabis banking law.

Washington application — flagged, not assumed: Washington merged its medical market into the LCB-licensed system in 2016; retailers with a medical endorsement sell to patients in the Medical Cannabis Authorization Database. Whether those endorsement sales count as “state-licensed medical” for Schedule III / 280E purposes is Verify — the federal rule text as located does not settle it. All adult-use activity remains Schedule I and fully subject to 280E. For tribal businesses under state compacts, whether any medical activity counts as “state-licensed” is also Verify (Part 10 adds a further flagged angle: 2015 Suquamish summaries report a renegotiation-on-rescheduling clause, but whether it survives in the Oct 2025 template / current compacts is Verify).

VERIFY / UNAVAILABLE means a point was not settled by an official page opened for this run, or official sources conflict. It is not a guess. Law points cite RCW / WAC text or official agency pages; market, polling and event points are secondary where labelled, with publication dates. Proposed, pending or failed bills are never presented as current law. No personal phone numbers or emails of individuals are reproduced; agency role mailboxes and the one DOR official public contact number that appear in Parts 5 and 9 are official public contacts as published, not personal details.

Place-name care: Vancouver WA is not Vancouver, British Columbia; Spokane WA is not Coeur d’Alene ID; Clarkston WA is not Lewiston ID; Walla Walla WA is not Milton-Freewater OR; Blaine WA is not Surrey BC. Same-name pairs elsewhere were kept apart throughout.

Parts 0–1 — Headlines & Laws

Washington Cannabis & Hemp — Run 1, Parts 0–1

Research date / cut-off: 1 November 2026. All facts: Accessed 2026-11-01 unless a different publication date is stated in the line.

How to read this report

  • Federal baseline is GIVEN and not re-researched: state-licensed MEDICAL marijuana and FDA-approved products moved to Schedule III effective 28 April 2026 (91 FR 22714); all adult-use marijuana remains Schedule I; IRS 280E is generally lifted for Schedule III medical activity and adult-use remains fully subject to 280E; hemp PL 119-37 §781 takes effect 12 November 2026 but PL 119-103 §2019 limits it until 11 December 2026, after which the full total-THC hemp definition and the 0.4 mg total THC per container cap apply; USDA enforcement of DEA-registered lab testing for hemp is delayed to 31 December 2026; the federal medical-marijuana appropriations rider runs through 11 December 2026; there is no federal cannabis banking law.
  • VERIFY / UNAVAILABLE means the point was not settled by an official page opened for this run, or official sources conflict. It is not a guess.
  • Law vs. secondary: Law points below cite RCW/WAC text opened via app.leg.wa.gov / apps.leg.wa.gov or LCB/DOH/WSDA official pages. Market figures, polling and event-type points are secondary and are labelled as such with publication dates. News, blogs and law-firm summaries were NOT used as authority for any law.
  • One failed fetch: a direct browser.open of RCW 69.51A.210 returned a tool/network error on this run. The same section's text was retrieved via the official apps.leg.wa.gov search result for that section and cross-checked against DOH's patient page and Justia's reproduction; it is quoted below and flagged where that matters. No other RCW fetch in this run failed in that way.
  • Same-name places: Vancouver, WA is not Vancouver, BC; Bellevue, Everett, Kent, Aberdeen, Auburn, Longview, Lakewood, Marysville, Richland, Pullman and Camas in Washington are not the same-named places in other states. Spokane WA / Coeur d'Alene ID, Clarkston WA / Lewiston ID, Walla Walla WA / Milton-Freewater OR and Vancouver WA / Portland OR are separate cities across state lines. No place-specific local rule is asserted in Parts 0–1 beyond the statewide buffer summary; full local detail belongs to Part 3.
  • Terminology change: Washington statutes and rules were recodified in 2022 (2022 c 16) from "marijuana" to "cannabis" in most places. Older sources saying "marijuana" and current sources saying "cannabis" are describing the same programme unless the text says otherwise.

PART 0 — HEADLINES

Headline What is current at 1 Nov 2026 Source + Accessed 2026-11-01
Market size — falling from peak, price-driven Licensed retail sales were about $1.14 billion in calendar 2025, down from about $1.18 billion in 2024 and about 21–22% below the 2021 peak of about $1.47 billion. Secondary reporting attributes the decline mainly to lower prices, not lower volume. Treat the $1.14B as secondary / DOR-via-trade-press, not as a figure read directly from a DOR spreadsheet on this run. MJBizDaily, "Washington cannabis sales slipped to $1.14 billion in 2025," published 2026-07-08, updated 2026-10-08, reporting WA Department of Revenue data: https://mjbizdaily.com/news/washington-cannabis-sales-fell-to-1-14-billion-in-2025/616818/ ; CannBus Q3 2026 market report summarising the same DOR series: https://www.cannbus.org/cannmir/wa_cannmir.html
Excise collected FY2025 $438,413,557 in FY2025 (July 2024–June 2025), down 3.6% from $454,742,324 in FY2024 (LCB FY2025 Annual Report). The LCB FY2025 Annual Report is the authoritative source to reconcile against in Part 2/6. LCB FY2025 Annual Report: https://lcb.wa.gov/sites/default/files/2025-12/2025%20annual-report-final-12-23-25-opt.pdf ; CannBus Q3 2026 summary of LCB FY2025: https://www.cannbus.org/cannmir/wa_cannmir.html
Total licences FY2025 1,604 total cannabis licences in FY2025 per LCB FY2025 Annual Report as summarised secondarily. A mid-2026 secondary count put licensed retail stores at 459 (DOH medical page, updated July 2026, via CannBus). Do not conflate "licences" (all types) with "open retail stores." LCB FY2025 Annual Report (above); CannBus Q3 2026: https://www.cannbus.org/cannmir/wa_cannmir.html
Retail cap The LCB-set retail maximum is 556. It was originally 334, raised by 222 in December 2015 after the Cannabis Patient Protection Act merger. 556 is a maximum, not a target that must be filled. LCB Fact Sheet: https://lcb.wa.gov/mj2015/fact-sheet ; WA Court of Appeals, Vision Research Group v. LCB, No. 55576-0-II (22 June 2022), summarising the 334→556 increase: https://www.courts.wa.gov/opinions/pdf/D2%2055576-0-II%20Unpublished%20Opinion.pdf
General-market moratorium The LCB is not accepting new producer, processor or retailer applications from the general public; the only new-licence route is the Social Equity Programme. Transportation, research and cooperative applications are described secondarily as open — Verify against the live LCB licensing page before advising an applicant. LCB Social Equity page: https://lcb.wa.gov/se/cannabis-social-equity ; CannBus Q3 2026: https://www.cannbus.org/cannmir/wa_cannmir.html
Social equity rollout Created by E2SHB 2870 (2020); expanded by SB 5080 (2023) to add up to 100 processor licences, up to 10 producer licences (from 1 Jan 2025, only with a processor licence) and up to 52 additional retail licences (2024–2032) plus forfeited/revoked/cancelled/unissued retail licences. LCB's own table shows 3 (FY23-24) + 6 (FY24-25) + 4 (FY25-26) + 3 (FY26-27) = 16 licences issued on that table; JLARC (July 2025) reported 9 retail licences issued as of Feb 2025; a Sept 2026 LCB board report was secondarily reported as 17 issued. Conflict flagged: 16 vs 17 depends on cut-off date and whether the count is retail-only or all types — use the LCB table as the headline and reconcile in Part 4. The June–July 2025 retail registration window drew 880 registrations; LCB reported 69 registrants "selected" as of 20 Oct 2025 (LCB Social Equity History page). The window is currently closed. Social-equity relocation/mobility took effect 1 Jan 2026 (secondary summary; statute/rule text to be confirmed in Part 4). LCB Social Equity: https://lcb.wa.gov/se/cannabis-social-equity ; LCB Social Equity History: https://lcb.wa.gov/se/history ; JLARC Cannabis Market Study, Proposed Final Report, July 2025: https://leg.wa.gov/jlarc/reports/2025/CannabisMarket/pf_ii/default.html ; LCB FY2025 Annual Report (880 registrations, June 2025 window): https://lcb.wa.gov/sites/default/files/2025-12/2025%20annual-report-final-12-23-25-opt.pdf
Licence fees rise 1 July 2026 Producer, processor and retailer issuance/renewal fees rose from $1,381 to $1,781 under EHB 2681 (Chapter 262, Laws of 2026, signed 1 April 2026), effective 1 July 2026, with CPI adjustment from 1 July 2027 (Seattle-area CPI-U). Social-equity licensees are temporarily fee-exempt through the programme sunset (see Part 1 §2). EHB 2681 House Bill Report (as passed House 28 Feb 2026, 52–42): https://lawfilesext.leg.wa.gov/biennium/2025-26/Htm/Bill%20Reports/House/2681.E%20HBR%20APH%2026.htm ; CannBus Q3 2026 summary: https://www.cannbus.org/cannmir/wa_cannmir.html
Tax — unchanged headline rate 37% retail cannabis excise (RCW 69.50.535) plus state sales tax 6.5% and local sales taxes. No 2025–26 restructuring to a weight/potency tax was enacted; 2026 excise-restructuring bills are reported secondarily as failed. Medical excise exemption runs to 30 June 2029 (see Part 1 §4). RCW 69.50.535 opened directly: https://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=69.50.535 ; DOR cannabis retailers page: https://dor.wa.gov/taxes-rates/taxes-due-cannabis/cannabis-retailers
Home grow — adult use still prohibited No adult-use home grow. Only qualifying patients / designated providers (and registered cooperatives) may grow at home. The 2025–26 home-grow bills (HB 1449 and SB 6204, both "up to six plants, 21+") did not become law: HB 1449 retained in House Appropriations; SB 6204 placed in Senate Rules "X" file 26 Feb 2026. HB 1449 status: https://app.leg.wa.gov/billsummary/?BillNumber=1449&Year=2025&Initiative=False ; SB 6204 status: https://app.leg.wa.gov/billsummary?BillNumber=6204&Year=2025&Initiative=false ; MPP 2026 legislation tracker (secondary, for "DEAD" summary): https://www.mpp.org/assets/pdf/issues/legislation/key-marijuana-policy-reform.pdf?v=1767475004
Intoxicating hemp — already channel-restricted Since 23 July 2023 (E2SSB 5367, ch. 365, Laws of 2023), any product intended to be consumed/absorbed inside the body with any detectable THC is a "cannabis product" saleable only by LCB licensees, and synthetically derived / completely synthetic cannabinoids are prohibited except FDA-approved products. General-retail delta-8, hemp delta-9 edibles/drinks and THCA smokable flower are therefore barred outside the LCB system. The coming federal total-THC / 0.4 mg per-container change (12 Nov / 11 Dec 2026) is therefore less disruptive inside Washington than nationally, because the state channel restriction already applies — but Washington has not yet enacted a conforming amendment to RCW 15.140 / 69.50.101 adopting the federal total-THC definition or per-container cap as state law as of this run: Verify against WSDA/LCB guidance after 11 Dec 2026. RCW 69.50.101 (opened): https://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=69.50.101 ; RCW 69.50.3251: https://app.leg.wa.gov/Rcw/default.aspx?cite=69.50.3251 ; LCB THC guide (May 2025): https://lcb.wa.gov/sites/default/files/2025-05/CIB-87-Products-Containing-THC-Guide_5625.pdf
Advertising liberalised 2026 ESB 5206 (ch. 378, Laws of 2025, effective 1 Jan 2026) expanded retail advertising; implementing WAC 314-55-155 amendments filed 3 June 2026 (WSR 26-12-082), effective 4 July 2026, raised exterior signs from 2 to 4 (each ≤1,600 sq in, entrance side) plus up to 2 trade-name signs. Older LCB FAQs still saying "two signs" are stale against the July 2026 rule. WSR 26-12-082: http://lawfilesext.leg.wa.gov/law/wsrpdf/2026/12/26-12-082.pdf ; WAC 314-55-155: https://apps.leg.wa.gov/WAC/default.aspx?cite=314-55-155 ; LCB advertising FAQ (stale on sign count): https://lcb.wa.gov/enforcement/cannabis_advertising_faqs
Traceability — CCRS is a stop-gap, replacement sought Current system is the Cannabis Central Reporting System (CCRS), an LCB-built reporting system (relaunched Dec 2021), not a full seed-to-sale traceability system. JLARC (July 2025): LCB "has not had a usable traceability system since 2017" and CCRS data are incomplete/unreliable. LCB sought $3.972M / 3.5 FTE for the Cannabis Traceability Project in the 2026 supplemental budget and about $12M / 8 FTE in the 2027–29 budget request (secondary report, Sept 2026). Verify funding outcome in Part 5/6. JLARC July 2025: https://leg.wa.gov/jlarc/reports/2025/CannabisMarket/pf_ii/default.html ; LCB petition response 20 Nov 2024 (history: BioTrack 2013–2017, Leaf 2017–, CCRS stop-gap): https://lcb.wa.gov/sites/default/files/publications/rules/2024-Proposed-Rules/Petition_Peterson11_20_24.pdf
Employment SB 5123 (2023), codified RCW 49.44.240, effective 1 Jan 2024: unlawful to discriminate in initial hiring based on off-duty cannabis use away from work or a test finding nonpsychoactive metabolites, with listed safety/federal exemptions. It does not protect current employees' off-duty use or bar post-accident / reasonable-suspicion testing. RCW 49.44.240: https://apps.leg.wa.gov/rcw/default.aspx?cite=49.44.240
Tribal compacts (headline only; full detail Part 10) Washington has 29 federally recognised tribes; a subset have cannabis compacts under RCW 43.06.490 (including Suquamish, Squaxin Island and Puyallup in the original cohort). Compact terms, tax-in-lieu, testing and trade rules are not summarised here beyond the Schedule III flag below, because Part 10 requires compact texts. Do not assume every tribe has a compact or permits sales to non-members. RCW 43.06.490 — Verify current compact list against Governor's Office / LCB compact page in Part 10; RCW 69.50.360(4) (tribal purchase by retailers) opened: https://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=69.50.360
Schedule III / 280E — Washington application (given baseline + state analysis) Medical endorsement sales: Washington merged medical into the LCB system in 2016 (SB 5052). A retailer with a medical endorsement selling DOH-compliant product to a recognition-card holder is selling under a state licence, but whether the federal Schedule III rule's phrase "state-licensed medical" covers endorsement retail sales (as opposed to a distinct medical licence, which Washington does not issue) is VERIFY — the federal rule text as summarised in the given baseline does not settle it, and no Washington guidance settling it was located on this run. Adult-use: remains Schedule I and fully subject to 280E — no change. Tribal medical activity under a state compact: VERIFY whether it counts as "state-licensed" for Schedule III / 280E purposes. Do not advise a licensee that 280E relief is secured on endorsement sales until that Verify is closed. Given federal baseline (see header); RCW 69.50.535(2) excise exemption (state tax only, not federal 280E): https://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=69.50.535
Major bills still open / dead (see §9) Enacted 2026: EHB 2681 (fees). Enacted 2025: ESB 5206 (advertising). Dead / inactive 2025–26: HB 1449 + SB 6204 (home grow); HB 1704 / SB 5547 (increase local revenue share — introduced 2025, no enactment found on this run, Verify final status); HB 2037 (modernising / residency removal + social-equity B&O exemption — introduced Mar 2025, Verify final status; do not present residency removal as law). Residency (6 months) therefore remains the stated rule in secondary summaries until a repeal is confirmed. Bill pages linked in §9 below.

PART 1 — LAWS

1.1 Legal status in one paragraph

  • Medical: legal since I-692 (approved 3 Nov 1998). Originally an affirmative defence only, with no licensed businesses; now a regulated programme inside the LCB system (SB 5052, 2015, effective 1 July 2016).
  • Adult-use: legal for adults 21+ since I-502 (approved 6 Nov 2012; first licensed retail sales 8 July 2014). Purchase only from LCB-licensed retailers (or tribal retailers under a compact, for the buyer rules in Part 10).
  • What an adult 21+ may do: buy and possess within the §3 limits below, give (not sell) limited amounts to another adult 21+ (see §3), and consume only in private / not in view of the public or in a public place.
  • What an adult 21+ may NOT do: grow cannabis at home without a medical authorisation; consume in public; drive impaired or with an open package accessible in a vehicle (see §3); sell without a licence; take cannabis across state lines or onto federal land (Part 10).
  • Federal overlay (given, not re-researched): adult-use remains Schedule I federally; medical Schedule III status from 28 Apr 2026 does not make adult-use federally legal and does not authorise interstate movement.

Verbatim operative text (opened pages; spelling/punctuation as in the official text)

RCW 69.50.101(4) — "Cannabis" (opened 2026-11-01):

"Cannabis" means all parts of the plant Cannabis, whether growing or not, with a THC concentration greater than 0.3 percent on a dry weight basis during the growing cycle through harvest and usable cannabis. "Cannabis" does not include hemp or industrial hemp as defined in RCW 15.140.020, or seeds used for licensed hemp production under chapter 15.140 RCW. Source: https://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=69.50.101 — Note: the page header on the fetch read "(Effective June 30, 2027.)" — Verify whether that is a future-effective version label or a display artefact; the definition quoted is the SB 5367 version in force for this report. Last-amended note on the page is via 2022 c 16 and 2023 c 365; confirm the current version date on the chapter page before filing.

RCW 69.50.101(8) — "Cannabis products" (SB 5367 version, opened 2026-11-01):

(a) "Cannabis products" means useable cannabis, cannabis concentrates, and cannabis-infused products as defined in this section, including any product intended to be consumed or absorbed inside the body by any means including inhalation, ingestion, or insertion, with any detectable amount of THC. (b) "Cannabis products" also means any product containing only THC content. (c) "Cannabis products" does not include cannabis health and beauty aids as defined in RCW 69.50.575 or products approved by the United States food and drug administration. Source: same RCW 69.50.101 page above.

RCW 69.50.101(5) — "Cannabis concentrates":

"Cannabis concentrates" means products consisting wholly or in part of the resin extracted from any part of the plant Cannabis and having a THC concentration greater than ten percent. Source: same page.

RCW 69.50.101(11) — "Cannabis-infused products":

"Cannabis-infused products" means products that contain cannabis or cannabis extracts, are intended for human use, are derived from cannabis as defined in subsection (4) of this section, and have a THC concentration no greater than ten percent. The term "cannabis-infused products" does not include either useable cannabis or cannabis concentrates. Source: same page.

RCW 15.140.020(7) — "Hemp" (opened 2026-11-01):

"Hemp" means the plant Cannabis sativa L. and any part of that plant, including the seeds thereof and all derivatives, extracts, cannabinoids, isomers, acids, salts, and salts of isomers, whether growing or not, with a delta-9 tetrahydrocannabinol concentration of not more than 0.3 percent on a dry weight basis. Source: https://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=15.140.020

RCW 15.140.020(8) — "Hemp consumable":

"Hemp consumable" means a product that is sold or provided to another person, that is: (a) Made of hemp; (b) Not a cannabis product, as defined in RCW 69.50.101; and (c) Intended to be consumed or absorbed inside the body by any means, including inhalation, ingestion, or insertion. Source: same page. Read with RCW 69.50.101(8): a hemp product with any detectable THC that is intended for consumption/absorption is a cannabis product, so it cannot also be a non-cannabis "hemp consumable" for general retail — that is the SB 5367 channel rule in statutory form.

RCW 69.50.360 — retailer sales to adults 21+ (opened 2026-11-01, full text):

The following acts, when performed by a validly licensed cannabis retailer or employee of a validly licensed retail outlet in compliance with rules adopted by the board to implement and enforce chapter 3, Laws of 2013, do not constitute criminal or civil offenses under Washington state law: … (3) Delivery, distribution, and sale, on the premises of the retail outlet, of any combination of the following amounts of cannabis concentrates, useable cannabis, or cannabis-infused product to any person 21 years of age or older: (a) One ounce of useable cannabis; (b) 16 ounces of cannabis-infused product in solid form; (c) 72 ounces of cannabis-infused product in liquid form unless the cannabis-infused product in liquid form is packaged in individual units containing no more than four milligrams of THC per unit; (d) 200 milligrams of THC within a cannabis-infused product in liquid form if the product is packaged in individual units containing no more than four milligrams of THC per unit; or (e) Seven grams of cannabis concentrate … Source: https://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=69.50.360 — Note the phrase "on the premises of the retail outlet" — it is the textual anchor for the no-delivery rule in §6.

RCW 69.50.535(1)(a) — 37% excise (opened 2026-11-01):

There is levied and collected a cannabis excise tax equal to thirty-seven percent of the selling price on each retail sale in this state of cannabis concentrates, useable cannabis, and cannabis-infused products. This tax is separate and in addition to general state and local sales and use taxes that apply to retail sales of tangible personal property, and is not part of the total retail price to which general state and local sales and use taxes apply. The tax must be separately itemized from the state and local retail sales tax on the sales receipt provided to the buyer. Source: https://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=69.50.535

RCW 69.50.535(2)(a) — medical excise exemption (opened 2026-11-01):

Until June 30, 2029, the tax levied by subsection (1) of this section does not apply to sales by a cannabis retailer with a medical cannabis endorsement to qualifying patients or designated providers who have been issued a recognition card, of cannabis concentrates, useable cannabis, or cannabis-infused products, identified by the department as a compliant cannabis product in chapter 246-70 WAC and tested to the standards in WAC 246-70-040. Source: same page. The 2024 act (ch. 79, Laws of 2024, HB 1453) created this exemption; JLARC must report on it by 1 Dec 2028 per the tax preference performance statement on the same page.

RCW 69.50.3251 — licence-required + synthetic ban (2023 c 365 §5):

(1) Except as otherwise provided in this chapter or as permitted under an agreement between the state and a tribe entered into under RCW 43.06.490, no person may manufacture, sell, or distribute cannabis, cannabis concentrates, useable cannabis, or cannabis-infused products, or any cannabis products without a valid license issued by the board or commission. (2) Except as permitted under an agreement between the state and a tribe entered into under RCW 43.06.490, any person performing any act requiring a license under this title, without having in force an appropriate and valid license issued to the person, is in violation of this chapter. (3) The producing, processing, manufacturing, or sale of any synthetically derived, or completely synthetic, cannabinoid is prohibited, except for products approved by the United States food and drug administration. Source: https://app.leg.wa.gov/Rcw/default.aspx?cite=69.50.3251 (also via Justia reproduction: https://law.justia.com/codes/washington/title-69/chapter-69-50/section-69-50-3251/)

RCW 69.50.445 — public consumption (see §3 for full text). RCW 69.50.331(8) — buffer (see §6 for summary; full text via Justia/official chapter page — open the chapter page to verify subsection numbering, as the legislature has renumbered this section over time). RCW 49.44.240 — employment (see §8 for full operative text).

The Code Reviser's "certified" dates visible on PDF versions in search results (e.g. 9 June 2026 for several sections) are certification dates, not amendment dates — do not cite them as "last amended."
  • WAC 314-55-079: WSR 26-14-119, filed 1 July 2026, effective 1 July 2026 (latest filing shown in search result); prior WSR 22-21-058 (filed 12 Oct 2022, eff. 12 Nov 2022).
  • WAC 314-55-155: WSR 26-12-082, filed 3 June 2026, effective 4 July 2026.
  • WAC 314-55-050 (premises/buffers): WSR 24-16-064, filed 31 July 2024, effective 31 Aug 2024 (per search-result filing note).
